Build QuantaKai from scratch

QuantaKai is a private assistant that runs entirely on a Windows laptop, from one folder you can move anywhere. It chats and remembers you, answers from your own books with page numbers, searches the web when you ask it to, writes stories and characters, runs the code it writes, reads pictures and documents, and talks and listens, all offline except Online mode. This guide lists every program, every download link, every folder, every command and every line of code needed to build it again from an empty folder.

1Before you start

You will end up withDetails
Chat modePersonal conversations that stream word by word, with long-term memory that learns facts about you
Offline modeAnswers only from your books and notes (PDF, EPUB, Word, PowerPoint, text), with clickable [1] sources and page numbers; 80 subjects
Online modeSearches the web (DuckDuckGo), reads the top pages, answers with numbered links
Create modeOriginal stories and full character sheets
Answer stylesDetailed (default), Quick, Code (with a Run button), Teach (full lessons), Quiz (interactive quiz, flashcards, saved study guide)
ExtrasPictures (Windows text recognition), documents attached to a chat, voice typing, answers read aloud while they are written, highlight any text to hear or explain it
Look and feelLight theme by default, dark theme, custom wallpaper, a nickname, and an animated mascot (the Blob) that dresses for each mode
ModelsAny .gguf model up to 5 GB, switched by hand on the Models page or the picker
PortableIts own Python, engine and models inside one folder; copy it to another drive or PC and it still runs
EffortAmount
Your timeAbout 2 to 3 hours by hand, or about 20 minutes with SETUP.bat once the files exist
DownloadsAbout 2.1 GB required (engine, Python, two models); about 230 MB more for voice; Kokoro voice about 160 MB more
Code29 files, 7,032 lines, all included in this guide

How to use this guide

  • Work from top to bottom. Each step ends with a tick box; your progress shows in the menu on the left (saved in this browser).
  • Commands go in PowerShell (Prepare Windows shows how to open it). Grey boxes have a Copy button; file boxes also have Save file.
  • Paths assume the folder C:\Users\Mulenga Mwamba\Desktop\QuantaKai. If you use another place, change only that start of each path.
  • Words in this style are typed exactly as shown: file names, commands, settings.
Rebuild protocol for AI assistants (read this first)
  1. Follow the sections in order. Do not skip a section because it seems obvious.
  2. Create the folders in Make the folders exactly, including capital letters and spaces. Then download the engine, the models and the portable Python as described.
  3. In Create every file, create every file in the listed order, copying each code box exactly: same text, same indentation (spaces, never tabs, in Python), same comments, same blank lines. Never write "...rest of code...".
  4. Save every file as UTF-8 without changing its name. Python files use 4-space indentation.
  5. Run the file checker (Check every file). Every line must say OK. Fix any file marked DIFFERENT by copying it again.
  6. Run the automatic test (Test without models). It must end with ALL TESTS PASSED.
  7. Only then do the First real run.
  8. If something fails, read the Troubleshooting section and the log files in logs before changing any code.

2How it fits together

Four kinds of program work together on the laptop. The browser page talks only to QuantaKai's own small web server; the server talks to two copies of the llama.cpp engine; optional voice programs run when needed.

Your browserShows ui\index.html at http://127.0.0.1:8000. You type, talk and click here.
⇄
QuantaKai app (portable Python)runtime\python runs app\server.py: finds memories, book passages, web pages and documents, builds the prompt, saves chats.
⇄
Chat engine :8090llama-server.exe + a chat model such as Qwen2.5-3B (1.93 GB). Writes every reply.
Search engine :8091llama-server.exe + bge-small (37 MB). Turns text into 384 numbers for searching.
ProgramStarted byPort / placeJob
Browserserver.py opens ithttp://127.0.0.1:8000The interface (one HTML file)
python.exe -m app.serverSTART.bat127.0.0.1:8000Web server, prompt building, search, memory, saving
llama-server.exe (chat)engine.py127.0.0.1:8090Runs the chosen chat model
llama-server.exe (search)engine.py127.0.0.1:8091Runs bge-small for vectors (--embeddings)
whisper-cli.exevoice.py, per recordingvoice folderSpeech to text (voice typing)
piper.exevoice.py, kept runningvoice\piperText to speech, fast
python.exe kokoro_worker.pyvoice.py, kept runningvoice\sherpa-onnx-...Text to speech, most natural (optional)
powershell.exe (OCR script)ocr.py, per picturedata\tmpReads text from pictures with Windows OCR

What happens when you press Send

  1. The page sends POST /api/chat with your message, the mode, the style and the chat's id.
  2. chat.py loads the chat from data\chats and gathers context: memories (Chat), book passages (Offline), web extracts (Online), attached documents (any mode), text read from pictures.
  3. It builds the messages list: system instructions from prompts.py, as much recent history as fits, then your message with its context.
  4. llm.py sends it to the chat engine with streaming on. Each piece of text goes straight back to the page as a Server-Sent Event.
  5. When the answer ends, the chat is saved. In Chat mode the model is then asked, in the background, which new facts about you to remember.

Design rules the code follows

  • Offline first. Nothing leaves the laptop except Online-mode searches and the one-time downloads.
  • Portable. Every path is worked out from the folder the code is in (settings.py ROOT, START.bat %~dp0, relative ._pth file).
  • Few dependencies. Only numpy and pymupdf beyond Python's own library. The page uses no frameworks and no internet fonts.
  • Safe by default. Listens on 127.0.0.1 only, checks Host and Origin headers, escapes all model text before showing it, runs code only when you press Run.
  • One reply at a time. A laptop has one engine; a lock queues requests instead of overloading it.
  • Never lose work. JSON files are written atomically; one bad book never stops the others; Stop keeps the text written so far.

3Words you'll meet

Every abbreviation and technical word in this guide, in plain English.

WordMeaning
AIArtificial Intelligence: software that does tasks that normally need human thinking.
LLMLarge Language Model: the kind of AI that reads and writes text (here Qwen2.5 3B).
ModelOne file holding everything an AI learned. Bigger is usually smarter but slower.
3B / 2B3 billion / 2 billion parameters (the numbers inside the model).
GGUFThe model file format llama.cpp uses. Every real GGUF file starts with the 4 letters GGUF.
Q4_K_MA quantization level: the model's numbers are stored in about 4 bits. The usual best balance of size and quality.
QuantizationShrinking a model by storing its numbers less precisely: smaller and faster, slightly less accurate.
llama.cpp / llama-serverFree program that runs GGUF models on ordinary computers; llama-server.exe is its web-server form, QuantaKai's engine.
mmprojMultimodal projector: a second GGUF file that lets some models see pictures.
AbliteratedA model edited so it stops refusing requests; usually a little less accurate.
TokenA word piece. Models read and write tokens; speed is tokens per second (tok/s).
Context windowHow many tokens the model sees at once: instructions + history + passages + its answer. Here 4096.
TemperatureRandomness when choosing words. Low (0.2) = careful and repeatable, high (0.9) = imaginative.
Prompt / system promptThe text sent to the model. The system prompt is the instructions part (prompts.py).
RAGRetrieval-Augmented Generation: find relevant passages first, then let the model answer from them. Offline and Online modes are RAG.
Embedding / vectorA list of numbers (here 384) that captures the meaning of a text. Similar meanings give similar lists.
bge-smallBAAI General Embedding, small: the tiny model that makes the vectors. BAAI = Beijing Academy of Artificial Intelligence.
CLS poolingHow bge turns a passage's word vectors into one: it takes the special first token's vector.
Dot product / cosineMultiply two vectors number by number and add up: the similarity score of two unit-length vectors (0 to 1).
BM25Best Matching 25: the classic keyword-search score. Rare words that appear often in a passage score high.
RRFReciprocal Rank Fusion: merges two ranked lists by adding 1/(60+rank) from each, so a passage ranked high by either rises.
Passage / chunkA piece of a book (about 180 words) that is searched and shown as a source.
OCROptical Character Recognition: reading text from a picture.
STT / TTSSpeech-To-Text (voice typing) and Text-To-Speech (reading aloud).
Whisper / whisper.cppOpenAI's open speech-recognition model, run by the whisper.cpp program.
PiperA fast offline text-to-speech program with downloadable voices (.onnx files).
Kokoro / sherpa-onnxKokoro is a natural-sounding voice model; sherpa-onnx is the program library that runs it.
ONNXOpen Neural Network Exchange: a common file format for AI models (used by Piper and Kokoro).
WAVA simple uncompressed sound file format.
CPU / GPU / iGPUCentral Processing Unit (the processor); Graphics Processing Unit; integrated GPU built into the processor (Radeon Vega 8).
VulkanA graphics programming interface; llama.cpp's Vulkan build can use the Vega 8.
RAMRandom Access Memory: the fast working memory. A model must fit in free RAM.
SSD / NVMeSolid-State Drive / Non-Volatile Memory Express: fast storage; models load in seconds.
PDF / EPUB / DOCX / PPTXBook and document file formats: Portable Document Format, Electronic Publication, Word, PowerPoint.
Python / embeddable PythonThe language the app is written in; the embeddable package is a complete Python in one folder that needs no installing.
._pth fileA text file beside portable Python listing where it looks for code. QuantaKai's has only relative paths.
Wheel (.whl) / pipThe file format Python packages come in (a zip file); pip is Python's package installer.
PyPIPython Package Index (pypi.org), where Python packages are published.
PATHThe list of folders Windows searches when you type a command.
PowerShell / .ps1 / .batWindows' command window; a PowerShell script file; a batch file of commands you double-click.
HTTP / API / JSONHow a browser and server talk; the set of addresses the page uses; a simple text format for data, like {"name": "Mwamba"}.
SSEServer-Sent Events: the server keeps the connection open and sends the answer piece by piece as 'data:' lines.
URL / localhost / 127.0.0.1A web address; your own computer. Addresses starting 127.0.0.1 never leave the laptop.
PortA numbered door on a computer: 8000 (page), 8090 (chat engine), 8091 (search engine).
PIDProcess ID: the number Windows gives each running program.
Job ObjectA Windows feature that groups processes; QuantaKai's job kills the engines when the app closes.
DNS rebindingAn attack where a web page tricks the browser into talking to apps on localhost. Blocked by checking the Host header.
CSRFCross-Site Request Forgery: another website quietly sending requests to a local app. Blocked by checking the Origin header.
HTML / CSS / JSHyperText Markup Language (structure), Cascading Style Sheets (looks), JavaScript (behaviour).
CanvasAn HTML element JavaScript draws on; the Blob mascot is drawn on one.
UTF-8The standard way of storing text characters. Save every file as UTF-8.
SHA-256Secure Hash Algorithm: a 64-character fingerprint of a file. Two files with the same SHA-256 are identical.

Big downloads

Downloads in a browser or Free Download Manager can be dropped into a folder named new stuff inside QuantaKai. At the next start, organize.py moves each one to the right folder (.gguf to models, voice files to voice) and voice.py unpacks voice archives automatically.

6The folder layout

QuantaKai lives in one folder. Everything it needs goes inside it. The folders marked (made by you) are created in Make the folders; the others are created by the app or by downloads.

folder tree23 lines
QuantaKai\
|-- START.bat  STOP.bat  SETUP.bat  VOICE_SETUP.bat
|-- config.json  requirements.txt  README.txt  BUILD_GUIDE.html
|-- app\            (made by you) the Python program: server.py, chat.py, library.py ...
|-- ui\             (made by you) index.html - the whole interface
|-- scripts\        (made by you) setup.ps1, setup_voice.ps1
|-- tests\          (made by you) smoke_test.py, fake_llama_server.py
|-- tools\          build_docs.py and friends (only to rebuild this guide), verify_files.py
|-- runtime\
|   |-- python\     portable Python 3.14 (python.exe, python314._pth, Lib\site-packages\numpy, pymupdf)
|   `-- wheels\     numpy and pymupdf .whl files
|-- engine\         llama.cpp: llama-server.exe and its .dll files
|-- models\         Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct-Q4_K_M.gguf, bge-small-en-v1.5-q8_0.gguf, other .gguf models
|-- books\          your PDFs, EPUBs, Word, PowerPoint, .txt and .md notes
|-- voice\          whisper-cli.exe + ggml-base.en.bin, piper\, Piper voices, sherpa-onnx + Kokoro (optional)
|-- new stuff\      drop downloads here; they are moved to the right folder at start-up
|-- data\           everything QuantaKai saves (never leaves the PC)
|   |-- chats\      one .json per conversation
|   |-- chat_docs\  documents attached to chats
|   |-- library\    books.json, categories.json, <book>.jsonl + <book>.npy per book
|   |-- tmp\        short-lived files (OCR, voice)
|   |-- memory.json  state.json  engine_pids.json  wallpaper.jpg
`-- logs\           chat-engine.log, embed-engine.log, setup_report.txt, voice_setup.txt
What goes on GitHub

Only the code and documents: app, ui, scripts, tests, tools, docs, the .bat files, config.json, requirements.txt and the read-me files. Never runtime, engine, models, voice, books or data: they are huge, downloadable or private. The .gitignore file enforces this.

7Prepare Windows

Show file extensions (do this first)

Windows hides endings like .bat and .txt. If they are hidden, an editor can save START.bat as START.bat.txt and it will not run.

  1. Open File Explorer (Windows+E).
  2. Click View, then Show, then tick File name extensions.

Open PowerShell

Press Windows, type PowerShell, press Enter. To open it inside a folder: open the folder in File Explorer, click the address bar, type powershell and press Enter. Windows already includes curl.exe (downloads) and tar.exe (unpacks .tar.bz2), which this guide uses.

A code editor

Visual Studio Code is free, colours code and saves UTF-8 automatically. Notepad also works: in Save As choose All files (*.*) and Encoding UTF-8.

8Make the folders

Run this once in PowerShell. The quotes matter because the user name has a space.

PowerShell - create the folders5 lines
$Q = "$HOME\Desktop\QuantaKai"
'app','ui','scripts','tests','tools','docs','runtime\python','runtime\wheels','engine','models',
'books','voice','new stuff','data','logs' | ForEach-Object { New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path (Join-Path $Q $_) | Out-Null }
Set-Location $Q
Get-ChildItem

9Download the engine (llama.cpp)

  1. Open github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/releases. New builds appear almost daily, named b + a number; any recent one works.
  2. Under the newest release click Assets, then download the file ending -bin-win-cpu-x64.zip. (The -vulkan-x64 file is only for the graphics test in the Speed section; CUDA files are for NVIDIA cards.)
  3. Right-click the zip, Properties, tick Unblock if it is there, OK.
  4. Right-click, Extract All…, into C:\Users\Mulenga Mwamba\Desktop\QuantaKai\engine. A sub-folder is fine; the app searches the whole engine folder.
PowerShell - check the engine (in the QuantaKai folder)2 lines
$exe = (Get-ChildItem .\engine -Recurse -Filter llama-server.exe | Select-Object -First 1).FullName
& $exe --version

You should see a version line with a build number. If SmartScreen appears, choose More info, Run anyway.

10Download the models

Models come from Hugging Face. Both required files go in the models folder with exactly these names. -C - resumes a broken download: just run the same line again.

PowerShell - download both models2 lines
curl.exe -L -C - -o models\Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct-Q4_K_M.gguf https://huggingface.co/bartowski/Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct-GGUF/resolve/main/Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct-Q4_K_M.gguf
curl.exe -L -C - -o models\bge-small-en-v1.5-q8_0.gguf https://huggingface.co/ggml-org/bge-small-en-v1.5-Q8_0-GGUF/resolve/main/bge-small-en-v1.5-q8_0.gguf
PowerShell - check the models (each must end with GGUF)4 lines
Get-ChildItem models\*.gguf | ForEach-Object {
  $b = [byte[]]::new(4); $f = [IO.File]::OpenRead($_.FullName); [void]$f.Read($b, 0, 4); $f.Close()
  '{0,-45} {1,8:N2} GB  {2}' -f $_.Name, ($_.Length / 1GB), ([Text.Encoding]::ASCII.GetString($b))
}

Optional now, to see the engine work before any code exists (open http://127.0.0.1:8090 in a browser, then press Ctrl+C in PowerShell to stop it):

PowerShell - try the engine on its own1 line
& $exe -m models\Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct-Q4_K_M.gguf --port 8090 -c 4096

11Set up the portable Python

Python's embeddable package is a complete Python in a zip file. Unzipped into runtime\python it runs without installing anything, and keeps working when the folder moves. It must be 3.14, because the numpy wheel is made for 3.14 (cp314).

  1. On www.python.org/downloads/windows/ pick the newest Python 3.14 release and download Windows embeddable package (64-bit), for example python-3.14.5-embed-amd64.zip. Direct link pattern: https://www.python.org/ftp/python/3.14.5/python-3.14.5-embed-amd64.zip.
  2. Extract it into runtime\python. You should see python.exe and python314._pth directly inside.
  3. Open python314._pth and replace everything in it with the five lines below. Save.
  4. Download the numpy wheel ending cp314-cp314-win_amd64.whl from pypi.org/project/numpy/#files and the PyMuPDF wheel ending win_amd64.whl from pypi.org/project/PyMuPDF/#files into runtime\wheels.
  5. Run the unpack-and-check commands below.
runtime\python\python314._pth5 lines
python314.zip
.
Lib\site-packages
..\..
import site

Line by line: the standard library zip; the Python folder; the packages folder; ..\.. = two folders up = the QuantaKai folder, so app can be imported; import site turns on normal package loading. All relative, so moving the folder is safe. (If your zip is named differently, e.g. python315.zip, use its name.)

PowerShell - unpack the wheels and check5 lines
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force runtime\python\Lib\site-packages | Out-Null
Get-ChildItem runtime\wheels\*.whl | ForEach-Object {
  runtime\python\python.exe -c "import sys, zipfile; zipfile.ZipFile(sys.argv[1]).extractall(sys.argv[2])" $_.FullName runtime\python\Lib\site-packages
}
runtime\python\python.exe -c "import sys, numpy, pymupdf; print(sys.version.split()[0], numpy.__version__, pymupdf.__version__)"

The last line must print three version numbers, for example 3.14.5 2.5.4 1.26.x.

Alternative with pip

Any installed Python can fetch matching packages straight into the portable one:
py -m pip install --target runtime\python\Lib\site-packages --platform win_amd64 --python-version 3.14 --only-binary=:all: -r requirements.txt

12Create every file

There are 29 files (7,032 lines). Create them in this order: each file only uses files that come before it. For every file: create it at the exact path shown, paste the whole code box (or press Save file and move the download into place), save as UTF-8.

For AI assistants rebuilding this project

Copy each code box character for character. Do not shorten, summarise, re-indent, rename, "improve" or translate anything, and do not merge files. Keep every comment. If a file is long, write it in several appends but never skip a part. After each file, compare its line count with the number in its header. When all files exist, run the checker in the section Check every file; it lists any file whose content differs.

#FileLinesNeeds firstWhat it is
1config.json32-Every setting in one place: names, ports, threads, the 5 GB model limit, model file names, search, answer lengths, voice.
2requirements.txt2-The two Python packages QuantaKai needs. Everything else uses Python's built-in library.
3app\__init__.py1-Marks the app folder as a Python package, so the files can import each other (from . import library).
4app\settings.py78config.jsonLoads config.json and defines every folder path. Every other module imports its paths and settings from here.
5app\prompts.py177-The written instructions the model receives before the user's message, one block per mode and style.
6app\engine.py338app\settings.pyStarts, stops and switches the two llama-server processes, and enforces the model size limit.
7app\llm.py121app\engine.pyTalks to the engines over HTTP with Python's built-in urllib (OpenAI-compatible API).
8app\library.py865app\settings.py, app\llm.pyThe book library: reads books and notes, cuts them into passages, builds vectors, sorts books into subjects, and searches.
9app\memory.py143app\llm.py, app\prompts.pyLong-term memory: facts about the user in data\memory.json, each with its search vector.
10app\web.py111-Online mode: searches DuckDuckGo's plain-HTML page, reads the top 3 pages in parallel and keeps the paragraphs that best match the question.
11app\runner.py80app\settings.pyRuns Python or Java code from an answer on this laptop (the Run button) and returns its output.
12app\ocr.py78app\settings.pyReads the text in a picture (a screenshot of a question, a photo of notes) with the text recognition built into Windows 10/11.
13app\docs.py429app\settings.pyDocuments attached to one chat: read once, saved beside the chat, and their most relevant parts sent with each question in that chat.
14app\organize.py67app\settings.pyTidies downloads: files dropped in a "new stuff" or "inbox" folder inside QuantaKai are moved to the right folder at start-up.
15app\kokoro_worker.py174-A helper program that keeps the Kokoro voice loaded in memory and turns each line of text it receives into a WAV file.
16app\voice.py608app\settings.py, app\kokoro_worker.pyOffline voice: speech-to-text with whisper.cpp, and reading answers aloud with Kokoro or Piper (Windows voices are the page's last resort).

1. config.json

Full path: C:\Users\Mulenga Mwamba\Desktop\QuantaKai\config.json32 lines968 bytesSHA-256 0eae207bcab3e6ea…

Every setting in one place: names, ports, threads, the 5 GB model limit, model file names, search, answer lengths, voice.

  • Plain JSON: double quotes only, no comments, no trailing commas. A syntax error stops the app at start-up.
  • threads: "auto" means "number of physical cores" (worked out in settings.py). A number forces it.
  • max_answer_tokens caps each answer kind (chat, quick, explain, code, teach, quiz, story, character, voice).
  • temperature has three keys: chat 0.7 (natural), study 0.2 (careful, used by Offline and Online), create 0.9 (imaginative).
  • Every key is explained in the Settings section of this guide.

4. app\settings.py

Full path: C:\Users\Mulenga Mwamba\Desktop\QuantaKai\app\settings.py78 lines2,817 bytesSHA-256 c389f95d0fd80279…

Loads config.json and defines every folder path. Every other module imports its paths and settings from here.

  • ROOT is the parent of the app folder, worked out at run time, which is what makes the folder portable.
  • Creates the books, models, engine, data, logs, data\library and data\chats folders if they are missing.
  • load_state()/save_state(): data\state.json holds settings the app changes itself (last model, auto-learn, nickname, wallpaper fade/blur, voice choice).
  • user_name(): the nickname from the Personalise page if set, else user_name in config.json.
  • write_json_atomic(): write a .tmp file, then os.replace it over the real one, so a crash never leaves half a file.

"""settings.py - loads config.json and knows where every folder lives.

Every other file imports CONFIG and the folder paths from here, so paths are
written in exactly one place.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
from pathlib import Path

# The project folder is the parent of this "app" folder.
ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent

BOOKS_DIR = ROOT / "books"      # your PDF books go here
MODELS_DIR = ROOT / "models"    # .gguf model files go here
ENGINE_DIR = ROOT / "engine"    # llama.cpp (llama-server.exe) goes here
DATA_DIR = ROOT / "data"        # everything the app saves (index, memory, chats)
LOGS_DIR = ROOT / "logs"        # log files for troubleshooting
UI_DIR = ROOT / "ui"            # the web page you chat in

LIBRARY_DIR = DATA_DIR / "library"   # one .jsonl (text) + one .npy (vectors) per book
CHATS_DIR = DATA_DIR / "chats"       # one .json file per conversation
MEMORY_FILE = DATA_DIR / "memory.json"
STATE_FILE = DATA_DIR / "state.json"  # remembers the last model you loaded
PIDS_FILE = DATA_DIR / "engine_pids.json"

for _folder in (BOOKS_DIR, MODELS_DIR, ENGINE_DIR, DATA_DIR, LOGS_DIR, LIBRARY_DIR, CHATS_DIR):
    _folder.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)


def _load_config() -> dict:
    with open(ROOT / "config.json", "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return json.load(f)


CONFIG: dict = _load_config()


def _auto_threads() -> int:
    """Real (physical) cores. Most AMD Ryzen and Intel chips show 2 'logical
    processors' per core; using more threads than real cores makes llama.cpp slower."""
    logical = os.cpu_count() or 4
    return max(2, min(8, logical // 2 if logical >= 8 else logical))


if str(CONFIG.get("threads", "auto")).lower() == "auto":
    CONFIG["threads"] = _auto_threads()


def load_state() -> dict:
    """Small settings the app changes itself (last model, auto-learn switch)."""
    try:
        return json.loads(STATE_FILE.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    except (OSError, ValueError):
        return {}


def save_state(**changes) -> dict:
    state = load_state()
    state.update(changes)
    tmp = STATE_FILE.with_suffix(".tmp")
    tmp.write_text(json.dumps(state, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
    os.replace(tmp, STATE_FILE)
    return state


def user_name() -> str:
    """What QuantaKai calls you: the nickname set on the Personalise page, else user_name in config.json."""
    nick = str(load_state().get("nickname") or "").strip()
    return nick or str(CONFIG.get("user_name", "there"))


def write_json_atomic(path: Path, data) -> None:
    """Write to a temporary file first, then swap it in, so a crash never leaves half a file."""
    tmp = path.with_suffix(path.suffix + ".tmp")
    tmp.write_text(json.dumps(data, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
    os.replace(tmp, path)

5. app\prompts.py

Full path: C:\Users\Mulenga Mwamba\Desktop\QuantaKai\app\prompts.py177 lines9,827 bytesSHA-256 b66c8f35126a10e0…

The written instructions the model receives before the user's message, one block per mode and style.

  • Placeholders {name} (app name) and {user} (nickname or user_name) are filled with str.format; CHAT_SYSTEM also has {memory_block}.
  • Offline rules (STUDY_*) force book-only answers with [n] citations and the exact refusal sentence "I couldn't find this in your books."
  • Online rules (WEB_*) do the same for web extracts. CODE_RULES, TEACH_RULES and QUIZ_RULES are shared by Offline and Online.
  • QUIZ_RULES fixes an exact text format (Q1. / A) / Answer: / Why:, then Card: x | y) that the page parses into an interactive quiz.
  • TYPO_NOTE is added to every system prompt; VOICE_NOTE only when the answer will be read aloud; STORY_RULES and CHARACTER_RULES drive Create mode.
  • MEMORY_EXTRACT asks the model, after a chat reply, for new facts as "- " lines or "- NONE".
app\prompts.py177 lines
"""prompts.py - the written instructions the model receives before your message.

Change the wording here to change the chatbot's personality or teaching style.
{name} and {user} are filled in from config.json.
"""

CHAT_SYSTEM = """You are {name}, a friendly personal assistant running privately on {user}'s own laptop.
Talk naturally, like a helpful friend. Be clear and direct. Use plain English.
When you use an abbreviation, say what it stands for the first time.
By default, answer like an expert: give a thorough, detailed explanation with the reasoning behind it, correct technical terms (explained the first time), concrete examples, and the important details, edge cases or common mistakes. Use headings or lists when they make a long answer easier to follow. Only keep it short if {user} asks for a short or quick answer.
What you CAN do: chat, explain ideas, help with study questions, write stories and create characters (Create mode), answer from {user}'s PDF books in Offline mode, and answer from web search results in Online mode.
What you CANNOT do: browse the internet, send emails or messages, set reminders, schedule tasks, open files or apps, or know today's date and news.
Never claim an ability that is not on the CAN list. If asked for something you cannot do, say so plainly.
If you are not sure of a fact, say you are not sure instead of guessing.
{memory_block}"""

MEMORY_BLOCK = """
Things you know about {user} from earlier conversations (use them only when relevant, never invent new ones):
{facts}"""

STUDY_RULES = """You are {name}, a patient tutor helping {user} study from their own books.
Use ONLY the numbered book passages given with the question. Do not use outside knowledge.
After each fact you take from a passage, put its number in square brackets, like [1] or [2].
If the passages do not contain the answer, say exactly: "I couldn't find this in your books."
Write clearly and in depth, at an expert level. Spell out every abbreviation the first time you use it."""

STUDY_QUICK = STUDY_RULES + """
Answer in 2 to 5 short sentences."""

STUDY_EXPLAIN = STUDY_RULES + """
Teach the idea properly, in this order:
1. A one-sentence answer.
2. A simple explanation, step by step.
3. A small everyday example.
4. Key terms explained in one line each.
5. A two-line summary."""

SIMPLER_REQUEST = ("Explain your last answer again, much more simply, as if I am a complete "
                   "beginner. Use a short everyday example.")

NO_PASSAGES = "(No passages in the books matched this question well.)"

MEMORY_EXTRACT = """Read this chat message from the user.
List any NEW facts about the user that are worth remembering for future conversations:
their name, studies, goals, plans, dates, preferences, people they mention, projects.
Ignore questions, small talk and anything about the assistant.
Write each fact as a short sentence starting with "- " and written about "the user".
If there is nothing worth remembering, write exactly: - NONE

User message: {user}

Assistant reply (for context only): {reply}

Facts:"""

WEB_RULES = """You are {name}, a careful assistant answering {user}'s question from web search results.
Use ONLY the numbered web extracts given with the question.
After each fact you take from an extract, put its number in square brackets, like [1] or [2].
If the extracts disagree, say so briefly. If they do not answer the question, say: "I couldn't find a clear answer online."
Write clearly and in depth, at an expert level. Spell out every abbreviation the first time you use it."""

WEB_QUICK = WEB_RULES + """
Answer in 2 to 5 short sentences."""

WEB_EXPLAIN = WEB_RULES + """
Give a one-sentence answer, then a clear step-by-step explanation, then a short summary."""

NO_WEB = "(No useful web results were found for this question.)"

CODE_RULES = """You are {name}, an expert programmer helping {user} write code.
Use the numbered passages given with the question as your guide: follow their methods, names and style,
and cite them like [1] when you rely on them. If they do not cover something, use your own programming knowledge
and say so in one line.
Reply in this order:
1. One or two sentences on the approach.
2. Complete, runnable code in a fenced code block with the language name, with clear comments.
3. A short explanation of how the code works and any important details or pitfalls.
4. An example of how to run or use it, with the expected output if it is useful."""

STUDY_CODE = CODE_RULES
WEB_CODE = CODE_RULES

TEACH_RULES = """You are {name}, an expert teacher giving {user} a real lesson.
Base the lesson on the numbered passages from {user}'s books and notes, and cite them like [1].
If the passages leave gaps, fill them with your own knowledge and say "(not from your books)" after that part.

If {user} wants to LEARN A TOPIC (for example a maths or science topic), teach it in this order:
1. What you will learn: 2 or 3 goals.
2. The idea in plain words, then the precise definition.
3. Key formulas or rules, each with what every symbol means.
4. Step-by-step explanation, building from simple to harder.
5. At least two worked examples, solved one step at a time.
6. Common mistakes and how to avoid them.
7. Three practice questions (easy, medium, hard). Do NOT give the answers; say {user} can reply with answers to get them marked.
8. A short summary.

If {user} wants to BUILD A PROJECT from notes, teach it in this order:
1. What the finished project does and what is needed (tools, prior knowledge).
2. The plan: numbered stages, from setup to finished project.
3. Each stage in turn: what to do, why, and the code or commands, with comments.
4. How to test that each stage works.
5. Common problems and fixes.
6. Ideas to extend it.

If {user} replies with answers to practice questions, mark each one, show the correct working, and explain any mistakes kindly.
If {user} says "next" or "continue", carry on from where the last lesson stopped."""

STUDY_TEACH = TEACH_RULES
WEB_TEACH = TEACH_RULES.replace("books and notes", "web sources").replace("(not from your books)", "(not from the sources)")

QUIZ_RULES = """You are {name}, an expert teacher making a revision quiz for {user}.
Base every question on the numbered passages and cite them like [1]. Cover the most important ideas.
Use EXACTLY this format and nothing else, so the app can turn it into an interactive quiz:

## Quiz: <topic>

Q1. <question>
A) <option>
B) <option>
C) <option>
D) <option>
Answer: <letter>
Why: <one or two sentences explaining the right answer> [n]

(write 6 questions like this, Q1 to Q6, getting harder; for maths include calculation questions)

## Flashcards
Card: <term or question> | <short answer or definition>
(write 8 cards)"""

STUDY_QUIZ = QUIZ_RULES
WEB_QUIZ = QUIZ_RULES

TYPO_NOTE = """

{user} often types quickly, so messages may contain spelling mistakes, missing words, wrong
word forms or broken grammar. Always work out what {user} most likely meant and answer that.
Do not point out or correct their spelling or grammar unless they ask you to. If a word is so
garbled that two meanings are equally likely, answer the most likely one and mention the other
in one short line. Always write your own answers with correct spelling and grammar."""


VOICE_NOTE = """

Your reply will be READ ALOUD, so talk like a person, not a document: answer in 2 to 4 short,
natural sentences. No headings, lists, tables, code or citation numbers unless {user} asks for them.
If the topic really needs more, give the short answer first and offer to go into detail."""

STORY_RULES = """You are {name}, a creative writing partner helping {user} write original fiction.
Write a vivid, original story from {user}'s prompt. Follow every instruction about genre, tone,
point of view, length, setting and characters. Where details are missing, choose interesting ones yourself.
Show, don't tell: use concrete sensory detail, natural dialogue and a clear sense of what each character wants.
Give the story a shape: a strong opening line, rising tension, and an ending or a deliberate cliffhanger.
Keep every character, name and fact consistent with anything already written earlier in this chat.
If {user} says "continue", "next" or "what happens next", carry on seamlessly from where the story stopped.
If {user} asks for changes (another ending, a darker tone, more dialogue), rewrite accordingly.
Unless asked for another length, write about 600 to 900 words. Give the story a title in bold on the first line.
Write only the story: no notes before or after it unless {user} asks. Never copy text from existing books."""

CHARACTER_RULES = """You are {name}, a creative writing partner helping {user} invent original characters.
From {user}'s prompt, build a complete, believable character. Invent anything not given, and keep it
consistent with characters and worlds already created earlier in this chat. Use exactly these headings:

## <Character name>
**Role:** their place in the story (hero, rival, mentor...) in one line
**At a glance:** age, appearance and the first thing people notice
**Personality:** 3 to 5 traits, including at least one real flaw
**Backstory:** a short paragraph: where they come from and what shaped them
**Wants and fears:** what they want most, what they fear most, and what they are hiding
**Voice:** how they talk, followed by 3 sample lines of their dialogue
**Skills and limits:** what they are good at, and what they cannot do
**Relationships:** key people in their life and how they feel about them
**Story arc:** how they could change over a story
**Story hooks:** 3 situations that would make a great scene for them

If {user} asks for changes, update the character and show the full sheet again.
Create original characters only; never copy characters from existing books, films or games."""

6. app\engine.py

Full path: C:\Users\Mulenga Mwamba\Desktop\QuantaKai\app\engine.py338 lines13,324 bytesSHA-256 30ce8c9b67ca298c…

Starts, stops and switches the two llama-server processes, and enforces the model size limit.

  • Two Engine objects: CHAT on chat_port (8090) and EMBED on embed_port (8091). States: stopped, loading, ready, error.
  • find_server_exe() searches the whole engine folder (any sub-folder). With the environment variable QUANTAKAI_FAKE_ENGINE set it returns the test engine instead.
  • list_models(): every .gguf in models except the search model and *mmproj* vision files; a file is allowed only if its first 4 bytes are GGUF and it is at most max_model_gb.
  • load_chat_model() starts llama-server with -c context_window -ngl gpu_layers --parallel 1, plus --mmproj when a matching vision file exists (find_vision_file).
  • start_embed_engine() runs bge-small with --embeddings --pooling cls -c 2048 -b 2048 -ub 512 --parallel 4 on embed_threads threads.
  • Start-up waits for GET /health to return 200 (chat: up to 240 s, search: 90 s). Output goes to logs\chat-engine.log and logs\embed-engine.log.
  • Windows Job Object with KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE: closing the START window kills the engines too.
  • data\engine_pids.json remembers the process numbers. kill_leftover_engines() checks the process NAME before killing, because Windows re-uses numbers.

app\engine.py338 lines
"""engine.py - starts and stops the llama.cpp AI engine (llama-server).

Two engines run side by side, each in its own background process:
  * the CHAT engine  - the language model you talk to (port 8090 by default)
  * the SEARCH engine - a tiny embedding model that turns text into numbers so
                        book passages and memories can be searched (port 8091)

Only one chat model is loaded at a time. Switching model = stop the old
process, start a new one with the other .gguf file.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import subprocess
import sys
import threading
import time
import urllib.request
import re
from pathlib import Path

from .settings import CONFIG, ENGINE_DIR, LOGS_DIR, MODELS_DIR, PIDS_FILE, ROOT, load_state, save_state

IS_WINDOWS = os.name == "nt"
# On Windows, start engines without opening extra black console windows.
NO_WINDOW = 0x08000000 if IS_WINDOWS else 0
GB = 1024 ** 3


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- helpers
def find_server_exe() -> list[str]:
    """Return the command that starts llama-server.

    Looks anywhere inside the engine folder, so it works whether you unzipped
    llama.cpp straight into engine\\ or into a sub-folder.
    The QUANTAKAI_FAKE_ENGINE variable is only used by the automatic
    tests (tests\\fake_llama_server.py) - you never need it.
    """
    if os.environ.get("QUANTAKAI_FAKE_ENGINE"):
        return [sys.executable, str(ROOT / "tests" / "fake_llama_server.py")]
    name = "llama-server.exe" if IS_WINDOWS else "llama-server"
    found = sorted(ENGINE_DIR.rglob(name))
    if not found:
        raise FileNotFoundError(
            f"{name} was not found inside {ENGINE_DIR}. "
            "Run SETUP.bat, or download llama.cpp by hand (BUILD_GUIDE.html, \"Download the engine\") and unzip it into the engine folder."
        )
    return [str(found[0])]


def is_gguf(path: Path) -> bool:
    """Every real model file starts with the 4 letters GGUF. Broken downloads do not."""
    try:
        with open(path, "rb") as f:
            return f.read(4) == b"GGUF"
    except OSError:
        return False


def http_ok(url: str, timeout: float = 1.5) -> bool:
    try:
        with urllib.request.urlopen(url, timeout=timeout) as r:
            return r.status == 200
    except Exception:
        return False


def list_models() -> list[dict]:
    """Every .gguf file in the models folder that can be used for chatting."""
    limit = float(CONFIG["max_model_gb"])
    embed_name = CONFIG["embedding_model"].lower()
    models = []
    for p in sorted(MODELS_DIR.glob("*.gguf"), key=lambda x: x.name.lower()):
        if p.name.lower() == embed_name or "mmproj" in p.name.lower():
            continue  # the search model and vision files are not chat models
        size_gb = p.stat().st_size / GB
        valid = is_gguf(p)
        reason = ""
        if not valid:
            reason = "Not a valid model file (download incomplete?)"
        elif size_gb > limit:
            reason = f"Bigger than the {limit:g} GB limit"
        models.append({
            "name": p.name,
            "size_gb": round(size_gb, 2),
            "allowed": valid and size_gb <= limit,
            "reason": reason,
        })
    return models


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- Windows job
def _make_kill_on_close_job():
    """Windows only: put engines in a "job" so they die when this app dies.

    Without it, closing the black START window would leave llama-server
    running in the background, still holding gigabytes of memory.
    Returns a function that adds a process to the job (does nothing elsewhere).
    """
    if not IS_WINDOWS:
        return lambda proc: None
    try:
        import ctypes
        from ctypes import wintypes

        k32 = ctypes.WinDLL("kernel32", use_last_error=True)
        k32.CreateJobObjectW.restype = wintypes.HANDLE
        k32.CreateJobObjectW.argtypes = [ctypes.c_void_p, wintypes.LPCWSTR]
        k32.SetInformationJobObject.argtypes = [wintypes.HANDLE, ctypes.c_int, ctypes.c_void_p, wintypes.DWORD]
        k32.AssignProcessToJobObject.argtypes = [wintypes.HANDLE, wintypes.HANDLE]

        class Basic(ctypes.Structure):
            _fields_ = [("PerProcessUserTimeLimit", ctypes.c_int64), ("PerJobUserTimeLimit", ctypes.c_int64),
                        ("LimitFlags", wintypes.DWORD), ("MinimumWorkingSetSize", ctypes.c_size_t),
                        ("MaximumWorkingSetSize", ctypes.c_size_t), ("ActiveProcessLimit", wintypes.DWORD),
                        ("Affinity", ctypes.c_size_t), ("PriorityClass", wintypes.DWORD),
                        ("SchedulingClass", wintypes.DWORD)]

        class IoCounters(ctypes.Structure):
            _fields_ = [(n, ctypes.c_uint64) for n in ("Read", "Write", "Other", "ReadBytes", "WriteBytes", "OtherBytes")]

        class Extended(ctypes.Structure):
            _fields_ = [("Basic", Basic), ("Io", IoCounters), ("ProcessMemoryLimit", ctypes.c_size_t),
                        ("JobMemoryLimit", ctypes.c_size_t), ("PeakProcessMemoryUsed", ctypes.c_size_t),
                        ("PeakJobMemoryUsed", ctypes.c_size_t)]

        job = k32.CreateJobObjectW(None, None)
        info = Extended()
        info.Basic.LimitFlags = 0x2000  # JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE
        k32.SetInformationJobObject(job, 9, ctypes.byref(info), ctypes.sizeof(info))

        def add(proc):
            try:
                k32.AssignProcessToJobObject(job, int(proc._handle))
            except Exception:
                pass
        add.job = job  # keep the job open for as long as the app runs
        return add
    except Exception:
        return lambda proc: None


_add_to_job = _make_kill_on_close_job()


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- PID file
def _read_pids() -> dict:
    try:
        return json.loads(PIDS_FILE.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    except (OSError, ValueError):
        return {}


def _write_pid(role: str, pid: int | None) -> None:
    pids = _read_pids()
    if pid is None:
        pids.pop(role, None)
    else:
        pids[role] = pid
    PIDS_FILE.write_text(json.dumps(pids), encoding="utf-8")


def _process_name(pid: int) -> str:
    """Name of a running program, e.g. 'llama-server.exe' ('' if it is not running)."""
    try:
        if IS_WINDOWS:
            out = subprocess.run(["tasklist", "/FI", f"PID eq {pid}", "/FO", "CSV", "/NH"],
                                 capture_output=True, text=True, creationflags=NO_WINDOW).stdout
            return out.split('","')[0].strip('"').lower() if out.startswith('"') else ""
        return Path(f"/proc/{pid}/cmdline").read_bytes().replace(b"\0", b" ").decode(errors="replace").lower()
    except Exception:
        return ""


def kill_leftover_engines(roles: tuple = ("chat", "embed")) -> None:
    """Stop programs this app started last time and left running.

    Windows re-uses process numbers, so the name is checked first: only
    llama-server (or python, for the app itself) is ever stopped.
    """
    pids = _read_pids()
    for role in roles:
        pid = pids.pop(role, None)
        if not pid:
            continue
        name = _process_name(int(pid))
        expected = "python" if role == "ui" or os.environ.get("QUANTAKAI_FAKE_ENGINE") else "llama-server"
        if expected not in name:
            continue
        try:
            if IS_WINDOWS:
                subprocess.run(["taskkill", "/F", "/T", "/PID", str(pid)],
                               capture_output=True, creationflags=NO_WINDOW)
            else:
                os.kill(int(pid), 9)
        except Exception:
            pass
    PIDS_FILE.write_text(json.dumps(pids), encoding="utf-8")


def remember_pid(role: str, pid: int) -> None:
    _write_pid(role, pid)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- engine
class Engine:
    """One llama-server background process."""

    def __init__(self, role: str, port: int):
        self.role = role            # "chat" or "embed"
        self.port = port
        self.proc: subprocess.Popen | None = None
        self.model: str | None = None
        self.state = "stopped"      # stopped | loading | ready | error
        self.vision = False         # True when the loaded model can look at pictures
        self.error = ""
        self.lock = threading.Lock()
        self.log = None

    @property
    def base_url(self) -> str:
        return f"http://127.0.0.1:{self.port}"

    def stop(self) -> None:
        if self.proc and self.proc.poll() is None:
            self.proc.terminate()
            try:
                self.proc.wait(timeout=8)
            except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
                self.proc.kill()
        self.proc = None
        if self.log:
            self.log.close()
            self.log = None
        self.state = "stopped"
        _write_pid(self.role, None)

    def start(self, model_file: Path, extra_args: list[str], timeout: float = 240,
              threads: int | None = None) -> bool:
        """Start llama-server with one model and wait until it answers /health."""
        with self.lock:
            self.stop()
            self.state, self.error, self.model = "loading", "", model_file.name
            log_path = LOGS_DIR / f"{self.role}-engine.log"
            cmd = find_server_exe() + [
                "-m", str(model_file),
                "--host", "127.0.0.1",
                "--port", str(self.port),
                "-t", str(threads if threads is not None else CONFIG["threads"]),
            ] + extra_args
            self.log = open(log_path, "w", encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
            self.log.write("COMMAND: " + " ".join(cmd) + "\n\n")
            self.log.flush()
            self.proc = subprocess.Popen(
                cmd, stdout=self.log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                cwd=str(ROOT), creationflags=NO_WINDOW,
            )
            _add_to_job(self.proc)
            _write_pid(self.role, self.proc.pid)
            deadline = time.time() + timeout
            while time.time() < deadline:
                if self.proc.poll() is not None:
                    self.state = "error"
                    self.error = f"The engine stopped while loading. Read logs\\{log_path.name}"
                    return False
                if http_ok(self.base_url + "/health"):
                    self.state = "ready"
                    return True
                time.sleep(0.5)
            self.state = "error"
            self.error = f"The engine took longer than {timeout:.0f} s to load. Read logs\\{log_path.name}"
            return False


CHAT = Engine("chat", int(CONFIG["chat_port"]))
EMBED = Engine("embed", int(CONFIG["embed_port"]))


def find_vision_file(model_name: str) -> Path | None:
    """A model's vision file ("mmproj") lets it look at pictures. It must be named after the
    model, e.g. Qwen3.5-2B-mmproj.gguf for Qwen3.5-2B-Q4_K_M.gguf (a wrong one crashes the engine)."""
    def norm(s: str) -> str:
        return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9.]+", "-", s.lower())
    stem = norm(Path(model_name).stem)
    family = re.sub(r"-(q\d.*|iq\d.*|f16|bf16|f32)$", "", stem)        # drop the quantisation part
    family = re.sub(r"-(instruct|chat|it)$", "", family)
    for p in MODELS_DIR.glob("*.gguf"):
        n = norm(p.stem)
        if "mmproj" in n and family and family in n:
            return p
    return None


def load_chat_model(name: str) -> bool:
    """Load a chat model by file name. Refuses files over the size limit."""
    match = next((m for m in list_models() if m["name"] == name), None)
    if match is None:
        CHAT.state, CHAT.error = "error", f"{name} is not in the models folder."
        return False
    if not match["allowed"]:
        CHAT.state, CHAT.error = "error", f"{name}: {match['reason']}."
        return False
    vision = find_vision_file(name)
    args = ["-c", str(CONFIG["context_window"]), "-ngl", str(CONFIG["gpu_layers"]), "--parallel", "1"]
    if vision:
        args += ["--mmproj", str(vision)]
    ok = CHAT.start(MODELS_DIR / name, args)
    CHAT.vision = bool(ok and vision)
    if ok:
        save_state(last_model=name)
    return ok


def start_embed_engine() -> bool:
    path = MODELS_DIR / CONFIG["embedding_model"]
    if not is_gguf(path):
        EMBED.state = "error"
        EMBED.error = f"Search model {CONFIG['embedding_model']} is missing from the models folder."
        return False
    # bge-small reads at most 512 tokens per passage and uses "CLS" pooling.
    return EMBED.start(path, ["--embeddings", "--pooling", "cls",
                              "-c", "2048", "-b", "2048", "-ub", "512", "--parallel", "4"],
                       timeout=90, threads=int(CONFIG.get("embed_threads", 2)))


def pick_startup_model() -> str | None:
    """Last model you used, else the default, else the first allowed model."""
    allowed = [m["name"] for m in list_models() if m["allowed"]]
    for candidate in (load_state().get("last_model"), CONFIG["default_model"]):
        if candidate in allowed:
            return candidate
    return allowed[0] if allowed else None


def stop_all() -> None:
    CHAT.stop()
    EMBED.stop()

7. app\llm.py

Full path: C:\Users\Mulenga Mwamba\Desktop\QuantaKai\app\llm.py121 lines4,743 bytesSHA-256 ef91433dfbaba37b…

Talks to the engines over HTTP with Python's built-in urllib (OpenAI-compatible API).

  • chat_stream(): POST /v1/chat/completions with stream: true, reads the data: {...} lines, yields {"text"} pieces then one {"stats"} (the engine's timings).
  • chat_once(): same endpoint without streaming, for background jobs (memory extraction).
  • embed(): POST /v1/embeddings, returns unit-length float32 rows. Questions get the bge prefix "Represent this sentence for searching relevant passages: "; passages do not.
  • Texts are cut to 1500 characters. If the engine rejects a batch (HTTP 400/413/500), each text is retried alone and shortened by 30% at a time until it fits.
  • Raises EngineNotReady when an engine is not in the ready state.

app\llm.py121 lines
"""llm.py - talks to the two llama-server engines over HTTP.

llama-server speaks the same "OpenAI-compatible" API that many AI apps use:
  POST /v1/chat/completions  -> the model writes a reply
  POST /v1/embeddings        -> text is turned into a list of numbers (a vector)
Only Python's built-in urllib is used, so there is nothing extra to install.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import urllib.error
import urllib.request
from typing import Iterator

import numpy as np

from .engine import CHAT, EMBED


class EngineNotReady(RuntimeError):
    pass


def _post(url: str, payload: dict, timeout: float):
    req = urllib.request.Request(
        url, data=json.dumps(payload).encode("utf-8"),
        headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"}, method="POST",
    )
    return urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout)


def chat_stream(messages: list[dict], max_tokens: int, temperature: float) -> Iterator[dict]:
    """Yield the reply piece by piece as it is written.

    Yields {"text": "..."} for each new piece, and finally {"stats": {...}}
    with the engine's own speed measurements.
    """
    if CHAT.state != "ready":
        raise EngineNotReady(CHAT.error or "No chat model is loaded yet.")
    payload = {
        "messages": messages,
        "max_tokens": max_tokens,
        "temperature": temperature,
        "top_p": 0.9,
        "stream": True,
        "stream_options": {"include_usage": True},
        "cache_prompt": True,
    }
    stats: dict = {}
    with _post(CHAT.base_url + "/v1/chat/completions", payload, timeout=600) as resp:
        for raw in resp:  # the server sends one "data: {...}" line per piece
            line = raw.decode("utf-8", errors="replace").strip()
            if not line.startswith("data:"):
                continue
            data = line[5:].strip()
            if data == "[DONE]":
                break
            try:
                event = json.loads(data)
            except ValueError:
                continue
            if event.get("timings"):
                stats = event["timings"]
            for choice in event.get("choices") or []:
                piece = (choice.get("delta") or {}).get("content")
                if piece:
                    yield {"text": piece}
    yield {"stats": stats}


def chat_once(messages: list[dict], max_tokens: int = 200, temperature: float = 0.1) -> str:
    """Get a whole reply in one go (used for background jobs like memory saving)."""
    if CHAT.state != "ready":
        raise EngineNotReady(CHAT.error or "No chat model is loaded yet.")
    payload = {"messages": messages, "max_tokens": max_tokens,
               "temperature": temperature, "stream": False}
    with _post(CHAT.base_url + "/v1/chat/completions", payload, timeout=300) as resp:
        body = json.loads(resp.read().decode("utf-8"))
    return body["choices"][0]["message"]["content"] or ""


# bge models search better when a QUESTION gets this prefix (passages do not).
QUERY_PREFIX = "Represent this sentence for searching relevant passages: "


def _embed_raw(texts: list[str]) -> list[list[float]]:
    with _post(EMBED.base_url + "/v1/embeddings", {"input": texts}, timeout=300) as resp:
        body = json.loads(resp.read().decode("utf-8"))
    return [r["embedding"] for r in sorted(body["data"], key=lambda d: d["index"])]


def _embed_one_safely(text: str) -> list[float]:
    """One passage that the engine refused (usually too many word pieces: code, formulas,
    tables). Keep shortening it until it fits, so one awkward page can't fail a whole book."""
    for _ in range(8):
        try:
            return _embed_raw([text])[0]
        except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
            if e.code not in (400, 413, 500) or len(text) <= 120:
                raise
            text = text[: int(len(text) * 0.7)]
    raise RuntimeError("A passage could not be indexed even after shortening it.")


def embed(texts: list[str], is_query: bool = False) -> np.ndarray:
    """Turn texts into unit-length vectors (rows). Similar meaning -> similar vectors."""
    if EMBED.state != "ready":
        raise EngineNotReady(EMBED.error or "The search engine is not running.")
    if is_query:
        texts = [QUERY_PREFIX + t for t in texts]
    texts = [t[:1500] for t in texts]  # usually inside the 512-token limit
    try:
        rows = _embed_raw(texts)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
        if e.code not in (400, 413, 500):
            raise
        rows = [_embed_one_safely(t) for t in texts]   # find and shrink the oversized one(s)
    vectors = np.asarray(rows, dtype=np.float32)
    norms = np.linalg.norm(vectors, axis=1, keepdims=True)
    norms[norms == 0] = 1.0
    return vectors / norms

8. app\library.py

Full path: C:\Users\Mulenga Mwamba\Desktop\QuantaKai\app\library.py865 lines45,242 bytesSHA-256 0734f1c12a656cac…

The book library: reads books and notes, cuts them into passages, builds vectors, sorts books into subjects, and searches.

  • Reads PDF and EPUB (PyMuPDF), Word .docx and PowerPoint .pptx (built-in zipfile + XML, slides keep their numbers and speaker notes), .txt and .md (400-word pages).
  • Passages: chunk_words (180) words with chunk_overlap_words (40) overlap, each remembering its first and last page.
  • Per book in data\library: <book_id>.jsonl (passages) and <book_id>.npy (vectors, row i = line i). books.json is the manifest; only new or changed files (size or time) are processed.
  • A queue and one worker thread add books one at a time; PROGRESS is read live by the Library page; a failed book never stops the others.
  • Subjects: 80 built-in subjects in 11 groups (SUBJECTS list). Title words decide first (trusted for technical subjects), otherwise the book's average vector is compared with each subject description. Your own choice (categories.json) always wins.
  • Search = hybrid: meaning (matrix @ question vector) + keyword (BM25, k1 1.4, b 0.75), fused with reciprocal rank fusion (1/(60+rank)). Overlapping passages from the same pages are skipped.
  • Auto subject picking scores each subject by its best 3 passages and keeps runners-up within 0.03 of the best.
  • Typo fixing for search only: a word not in the books is replaced by the most common book word 1 (or 2) edits away.

app\library.py865 lines
"""library.py - reads your PDF books, cuts them into passages and searches them.

How a book is added:
  1. PyMuPDF pulls the text out of every page.
  2. The text is cut into overlapping passages of about 180 words.
  3. The search engine turns each passage into a vector (384 numbers).
  4. Two files are saved per book in data\\library\\:
        <book_id>.jsonl  - the passages (one per line, with page numbers)
        <book_id>.npy    - the vectors, row i belongs to line i
Only NEW or CHANGED PDFs are processed, so adding book 21 does not redo books 1-20.

How a question is searched:
  The question becomes a vector too. Comparing it with every passage vector
  (a "dot product") gives a similarity score; the highest scores win.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import json
import re
import threading
import time
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np

from . import llm
from .settings import BOOKS_DIR, CONFIG, LIBRARY_DIR, write_json_atomic

MANIFEST = LIBRARY_DIR / "books.json"
_lock = threading.Lock()

# Live progress, read by the web page while books are being added.
PROGRESS = {"running": False, "book": "", "done_books": 0, "total_books": 0,
            "step": "", "percent": 0, "messages": []}


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- manifest
def load_manifest() -> dict:
    try:
        return json.loads(MANIFEST.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    except (OSError, ValueError):
        return {}


def book_id_for(pdf: Path) -> str:
    slug = re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", "-", pdf.stem.lower()).strip("-")[:40] or "book"
    short = hashlib.sha1(pdf.name.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()[:6]
    return f"{slug}-{short}"


def pretty_title(pdf: Path, meta_title: str | None) -> str:
    if meta_title and len(meta_title.strip()) > 3:
        return meta_title.strip()
    name = re.sub(r"(?i)\((?:z-library|z-lib|1lib)[^)]*\)", "", pdf.stem)   # "(z-library.sk, ...)"
    name = re.sub(r"(?i)oceanofpdf\.?com", "", name)
    name = re.sub(r"[_\-]+", " ", name)
    name = re.sub(r"(?i)\b\d+nbsped\b|\b97[89]\d{10}\b|\b\d{9}[\dx]\b|\bcompress(ed)?\b|\bfinal release\b", "", name)
    name = re.sub(r"(?<=[a-z])(?=[A-Z])", " ", name)                       # JavaCompleteReference
    name = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", name).strip() or pdf.stem
    if name == name.lower():                                                 # all lower case -> Title Case
        small = {"a", "an", "and", "the", "of", "to", "for", "in", "with", "on"}
        name = " ".join(w if (i and w in small) else w.capitalize() for i, w in enumerate(name.split()))
    return name


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- reading
SOURCE_TYPES = (".pdf", ".epub", ".txt", ".md", ".docx", ".pptx")   # books AND your own notes

_W = "{http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/wordprocessingml/2006/main}"
_A = "{http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/drawingml/2006/main}"


def _pages_from_text(raw: str, words_per_page: int = 400) -> list[tuple[int, str]]:
    """Cut plain text into 'pages' of about 400 words, keeping whole lines together."""
    pages, buf, count = [], [], 0
    for line in raw.splitlines(keepends=True):
        buf.append(line); count += len(line.split())
        if count >= words_per_page:
            pages.append((len(pages) + 1, "".join(buf).strip())); buf, count = [], 0
    if "".join(buf).strip():
        pages.append((len(pages) + 1, "".join(buf).strip()))
    return pages


def _office_title(z) -> str | None:
    """The title saved inside a Word or PowerPoint file (File > Info > Title), if any."""
    import xml.etree.ElementTree as ET
    try:
        root = ET.fromstring(z.read("docProps/core.xml"))
        el = root.find("{http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/}title")
        return (el.text or "").strip() or None if el is not None else None
    except (KeyError, ET.ParseError):
        return None


def _docx_pages(path: Path) -> tuple[list[tuple[int, str]], str | None]:
    """Word (.docx): every paragraph and table cell, in order. Uses only built-in Python."""
    import xml.etree.ElementTree as ET
    import zipfile
    with zipfile.ZipFile(path) as z:
        root = ET.fromstring(z.read("word/document.xml"))
        title = _office_title(z)
    lines = []
    for p in root.iter(_W + "p"):
        parts = []
        for el in p.iter():
            if el.tag == _W + "t" and el.text:
                parts.append(el.text)
            elif el.tag == _W + "tab":
                parts.append("\t")
            elif el.tag in (_W + "br", _W + "cr"):
                parts.append("\n")
        line = "".join(parts).strip()
        if line:
            lines.append(line)
    return _pages_from_text("\n".join(lines) + "\n"), title


def _pptx_pages(path: Path) -> tuple[list[tuple[int, str]], str | None]:
    """PowerPoint (.pptx): one 'page' per slide (so page 3 = slide 3), with the speaker notes."""
    import posixpath
    import xml.etree.ElementTree as ET
    import zipfile

    def slide_text(xml: bytes) -> str:
        root = ET.fromstring(xml)
        paras = ["".join(t.text or "" for t in p.iter(_A + "t")).strip() for p in root.iter(_A + "p")]
        return "\n".join(x for x in paras if x)

    with zipfile.ZipFile(path) as z:
        names = set(z.namelist())
        slides = sorted((n for n in names if re.fullmatch(r"ppt/slides/slide\d+\.xml", n)),
                        key=lambda n: int(re.search(r"(\d+)\.xml$", n).group(1)))
        pages = []
        for n in slides:
            text = slide_text(z.read(n))
            rels = n.replace("ppt/slides/", "ppt/slides/_rels/") + ".rels"
            if rels in names:   # find this slide's speaker notes, if it has any
                m = re.search(rb'Target="([^"]*notesSlide\d+\.xml)"', z.read(rels))
                if m:
                    target = posixpath.normpath(posixpath.join("ppt/slides", m.group(1).decode()))
                    if target in names:
                        notes = slide_text(z.read(target))
                        notes = re.sub(r"^\d+$", "", notes, flags=re.M).strip()   # drop the slide-number box
                        if notes:
                            text += "\nSpeaker notes: " + notes
            number = int(re.search(r"(\d+)\.xml$", n).group(1))
            pages.append((number, text))
        title = _office_title(z)
    return pages, title


def source_files() -> list[Path]:
    """Every readable file in the books folder: PDF/EPUB books and .txt/.md notes."""
    return [p for p in BOOKS_DIR.iterdir() if p.is_file() and p.suffix.lower() in SOURCE_TYPES]


def extract_pages(pdf: Path) -> tuple[list[tuple[int, str]], str | None]:
    """Return [(page_number, text), ...] using 1-based PDF page numbers.
    Plain-text notes (.txt/.md) and Word files are cut into 'pages' of about 400 words;
    PowerPoint files use one page per slide."""
    kind = pdf.suffix.lower()
    if kind in (".txt", ".md"):
        return _pages_from_text(pdf.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")), None
    if kind == ".docx":
        return _docx_pages(pdf)
    if kind == ".pptx":
        return _pptx_pages(pdf)
    import pymupdf  # imported here so the rest of the app starts even if it is missing

    pages = []
    with pymupdf.open(pdf) as doc:
        meta_title = (doc.metadata or {}).get("title")
        for i, page in enumerate(doc, start=1):
            text = page.get_text("text") or ""
            text = re.sub(r"-\n(?=[a-z])", "", text)        # join words split by hyphen at line end
            text = re.sub(r"[ \t]+", " ", text)
            text = re.sub(r"\n{2,}", "\n\n", text).strip()
            pages.append((i, text))
    return pages, meta_title


def make_passages(book_id: str, pages: list[tuple[int, str]]) -> list[dict]:
    """Cut the whole book into overlapping windows of words, remembering page numbers."""
    size = int(CONFIG["chunk_words"])
    step = max(20, size - int(CONFIG["chunk_overlap_words"]))
    words: list[str] = []
    word_page: list[int] = []
    for page_no, text in pages:
        for w in text.split():
            words.append(w)
            word_page.append(page_no)
    passages = []
    for start in range(0, len(words), step):
        chunk = words[start:start + size]
        if len(chunk) < 25 and passages:   # tiny leftover at the end: skip
            break
        passages.append({
            "id": f"{book_id}-{len(passages):05d}",
            "book_id": book_id,
            "page": word_page[start],
            "page_end": word_page[min(start + len(chunk), len(words)) - 1],
            "text": " ".join(chunk),
        })
        if start + size >= len(words):
            break
    return passages
